5a:T589,<p>The ECMAScript language implicitly performs automatic type conversion as needed. To clarify the semantics of certain constructs it is useful to define a set of conversion <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref>. The conversion <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> are polymorphic; they can accept a value of any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language type</a></emu-xref>. But no other specification types are used with these operations.</p>
    <p>The <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-bigint-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types-bigint-type">BigInt type</a></emu-xref> has no implicit conversions in the ECMAScript language; programmers must call BigInt explicitly to convert values from other types.</p>

    5b:T122b,<p>The abstract operation ToPrimitive takes argument <var>input</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>) and optional argument <var>preferredType</var> (<emu-const>string</emu-const> or <emu-const>number</emu-const>) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>. It converts its <var>input</var> argument to a non-<emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">Object type</a></emu-xref>. If an object is capable of converting to more than one primitive type, it may use the optional hint <var>preferredType</var> to favour that type. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
      <emu-alg><ol><li>If <var>input</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is an Object</a></emu-xref>, then<ol><li>Let <var>exoticToPrimitive</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="GetMethod"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-getmethod">GetMethod</a></emu-xref>(<var>input</var>, <emu-intrinsic>%Symbol.toPrimitive%</emu-intrinsic>).</li><li>If <var>exoticToPrimitive</var> is not <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, then<ol><li>If <var>preferredType</var> is not present, then<ol><li>Let <var>hint</var> be <emu-val>"default"</emu-val>.</li></ol></li><li>Else if <var>preferredType</var> is <emu-const>string</emu-const>, then<ol><li>Let <var>hint</var> be <emu-val>"string"</emu-val>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>preferredType</var> is <emu-const>number</emu-const>.</li><li>Let <var>hint</var> be <emu-val>"number"</emu-val>.</li></ol></li><li>Let <var>result</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="Call"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-call">Call</a></emu-xref>(<var>exoticToPrimitive</var>, <var>input</var>, « <var>hint</var> »).</li><li class="exit">If <var>result</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is not an Object</a></emu-xref>, return <var>result</var>.</li><li class="exit">Throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception.</li></ol></li><li>If <var>preferredType</var> is not present, set <var>preferredType</var> to <emu-const>number</emu-const>.</li><li class="exit">Return ? <emu-xref aoid="OrdinaryToPrimitive"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-ordinarytoprimitive">OrdinaryToPrimitive</a></emu-xref>(<var>input</var>, <var>preferredType</var>).</li></ol></li><li class="exit">Return <var>input</var>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>When ToPrimitive is called without a hint, then it generally behaves as if the hint were <emu-const>number</emu-const>. However, objects may over-ride this behaviour by defining a <emu-intrinsic>%Symbol.toPrimitive%</emu-intrinsic> method. Of the objects defined in this specification only Dates (see <emu-xref href="#sec-date.prototype-%symbol.toprimitive%"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-date.prototype-%symbol.toprimitive%">21.4.4.45</a></emu-xref>) and Symbol objects (see <emu-xref href="#sec-symbol.prototype-%symbol.toprimitive%"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/fundamental-objects#sec-symbol.prototype-%symbol.toprimitive%">20.4.3.5</a></emu-xref>) over-ride the default ToPrimitive behaviour. Dates treat the absence of a hint as if the hint were <emu-const>string</emu-const>.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

79:T8f5,<p>The abstract operation OrdinaryToPrimitive takes arguments <var>obj</var> (an Object) and <var>hint</var> (<emu-const>string</emu-const> or <emu-const>number</emu-const>) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
        <emu-alg><ol><li>If <var>hint</var> is <emu-const>string</emu-const>, then<ol><li>Let <var>methodNames</var> be « <emu-val>"toString"</emu-val>, <emu-val>"valueOf"</emu-val> ».</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>Let <var>methodNames</var> be « <emu-val>"valueOf"</emu-val>, <emu-val>"toString"</emu-val> ».</li></ol></li><li>For each element <var>name</var> of <var>methodNames</var>, do<ol><li>Let <var>method</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="Get"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-get-o-p">Get</a></emu-xref>(<var>obj</var>, <var>name</var>).</li><li>If <emu-xref aoid="IsCallable"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-iscallable">IsCallable</a></emu-xref>(<var>method</var>) is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>result</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="Call"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-call">Call</a></emu-xref>(<var>method</var>, <var>obj</var>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>result</var> <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-object-type">is not an Object</a></emu-xref>, return <var>result</var>.</li></ol></li></ol></li><li class="exit">Throw a <emu-val>TypeError</emu-val> exception.</li></ol></emu-alg>

7a:T8c2,<p>The abstract operation ToBoolean takes argument <var>arg</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>) and returns a Boolean.       <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>ToFixedSizeInteger is idempotent: for any <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref> <var>x</var>, ToFixedSizeInteger(ToFixedSizeInteger(<var>x</var>, <var>signed</var>, <var>bitWidth</var>), <var>signed</var>, <var>bitWidth</var>) is the same as ToFixedSizeInteger(<var>x</var>, <var>signed</var>, <var>bitWidth</var>). In fact, because +∞ and -∞ are mapped to 0, this invariant even survives inversion of <var>signed</var> (e.g., ToFixedSizeInteger(ToFixedSizeInteger(<var>x</var>, <emu-const>signed</emu-const>, 32), <emu-const>unsigned</emu-const>, 32) is the same as ToFixedSizeInteger(<var>x</var>, <emu-const>unsigned</emu-const>, 32)).</p>

      <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>number</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToNumber"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-tonumber">ToNumber</a></emu-xref>(<var>arg</var>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>number</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>, return <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.</li><li>Let <var>mv</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#extended-mathematical-value-of"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#extended-mathematical-value-of">extended mathematical value of</a></emu-xref> <var>number</var>.</li><li>Let <var>clamped</var> be the result of <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#clamping"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#clamping">clamping</a></emu-xref> <var>mv</var> between 0 and 255.</li><li>Let <var>f</var> be <emu-xref aoid="floor"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#eqn-floor">floor</a></emu-xref>(<var>clamped</var>).</li><li>If <var>clamped</var> &lt; <var>f</var> + 0.5, return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>f</var>).</li><li>If <var>clamped</var> > <var>f</var> + 0.5, return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>f</var> + 1).</li><li>If <var>f</var> is even, return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>f</var>).</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>f</var> + 1).</li></ol></emu-alg>
      <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Unlike most other ECMAScript <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref> conversion operations, ToUint8Clamp rounds rather than truncates non-integral values. It also uses “round half to even” tie-breaking, which differs from the “round half up” tie-breaking of <emu-xref href="#sec-math.round"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/numbers-and-dates#sec-math.round"><code>Math.round</code></a></emu-xref>.</p>

88:T56d,<p>The abstract operation StringToBigInt takes argument <var>string</var> (a String) and returns a BigInt or <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
      <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>literal</var> be <emu-xref aoid="ParseText"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-source-code#sec-parsetext">ParseText</a></emu-xref>(<var>string</var>, <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringIntegerLiteral">StringIntegerLiteral</a></emu-nt>).</li><li class="exit">If <var>literal</var> is a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of errors, return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.</li><li>Let <var>mv</var> be the MV of <var>literal</var>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: <var>mv</var> is an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer">integer</a></emu-xref>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="ℤ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℤ">ℤ</a></emu-xref>(<var>mv</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>

      89:T6a7,<p><emu-xref aoid="StringToBigInt"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-stringtobigint">StringToBigInt</a></emu-xref> uses the following grammar.</p>
        <h2>Syntax</h2>

a1:T14c2,<p>The abstract operation MakeBasicObject takes argument <var>internalSlotsList</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-list-and-record-specification-type">List</a></emu-xref> of internal slot names) and returns an Object. It is the source of all ECMAScript objects that are created algorithmically, including both <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#ordinary-object">ordinary objects</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref>. It factors out common steps used in creating all objects, and centralizes object creation. It performs the following steps when called:</p>

      <emu-alg><ol><li>Set <var>internalSlotsList</var> to the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#list-concatenation"><a <var class="field">[[GetPrototypeOf]]</var> and <var class="field">[[SetPrototypeOf]]</var> essential internal methods, then <var>internalSlotsList</var> contains <var class="field">[[Prototype]]</var>.</li><li><emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#assert">Assert</a></emu-xref>: If the essential internal methods, then <var>internalSlotsList</var> contains <var class="field">[[Extensible]]</var>.</li><li>If <var>internalSlotsList</var> contains <var       <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Within this specification, <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic objects</a></emu-xref> are created in <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#sec-algorithm-conventions-abstract-operations">abstract operations</a></emu-xref> such as <emu-xref aoid="ArrayCreate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-arraycreate">ArrayCreate</a></emu-xref> and <emu-xref aoid="BoundFunctionCreate"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ordinary-and-exotic-objects-behaviours#sec-boundfunctioncreate">BoundFunctionCreate</a></emu-xref> by first calling MakeBasicObject to obtain a basic, foundational object, and then overriding some or all of that object's internal methods. In order to encapsulate <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#exotic-object">exotic object</a></emu-xref> creation, the object's essential internal methods are never modified outside those operations.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

d9:T1eb9,<p>The abstract operation <emu-xref aoid="StringToNumber"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-stringtonumber">StringToNumber</a></emu-xref> specifies how to convert a String value to a Number value, using the following grammar.</p>
        <h2>Syntax</h2>
        <emu-grammar type="definition"><emu-production id="prod-StringNumericLiteral" name="StringNumericLiteral"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpace">StrWhiteSpace</a><emu-mods><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpace">StrWhiteSpace</a><emu-mods><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrNumericLiteral">StrNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpace">StrWhiteSpace</a><emu-mods><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-StrWhiteSpace" name="StrWhiteSpace"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpace">StrWhiteSpace</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpaceChar">StrWhiteSpaceChar</a></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpace">StrWhiteSpace</a><emu-mods><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-StrWhiteSpaceChar" name="StrWhiteSpaceChar"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrWhiteSpaceChar">StrWhiteSpaceChar</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-WhiteSpace">WhiteSpace</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-LineTerminator">LineTerminator</a></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production>

<emu-production id="prod-StrUnsignedDecimalLiteral" name="StrUnsignedDecimalLiteral"><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StrUnsignedDecimalLiteral">StrUnsignedDecimalLiteral</a></emu-nt> <emu-geq>:::</emu-geq>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>Infinity</emu-t></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-t>.</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-ExponentPart">ExponentPart</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-t>.</emu-t> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-ExponentPart">ExponentPart</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs>  <emu-rhs><emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params></emu-mods></emu-nt> <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-ExponentPart">ExponentPart</a><emu-mods><emu-params>[~Sep]</emu-params><emu-opt>opt</emu-opt></emu-mods></emu-nt></emu-rhs></emu-production></emu-grammar>
        <p>All grammar symbols not explicitly defined above have the definitions used in the Lexical Grammar for numeric literals (<emu-xref href="#sec-literals-numeric-literals"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#sec-literals-numeric-literals">12.9.3</a></emu-xref>)</p>
        <emu-note><span class="note">Note</span><div class="note-contents">
          <p>Some differences should be noted between the syntax of a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> and a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-NumericLiteral">NumericLiteral</a></emu-nt>:</p>
          <ul>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> may include leading and/or trailing white space and/or line terminators.
            </li>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> that is decimal may have any number of leading <code>0</code> digits.
            </li>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> that is decimal may include a <code>+</code> or <code>-</code> to indicate its sign.
            </li>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> that is empty or contains only white space is converted to <emu-val>+0</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>.
            </li>
            <li>
              <code>Infinity</code> and <code>-Infinity</code> are recognized as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> but not as a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-NumericLiteral">NumericLiteral</a></emu-nt>.
            </li>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> cannot include a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-BigIntLiteralSuffix">BigIntLiteralSuffix</a></emu-nt>.
            </li>
            <li>
              A <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#prod-StringNumericLiteral">StringNumericLiteral</a></emu-nt> cannot include a <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-NumericLiteralSeparator">NumericLiteralSeparator</a></emu-nt>.
            </li>
          </ul>
        </div></emu-note>

e5:T3167,<p>The abstract operation IsLessThan takes arguments <var>x</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>), <var>y</var> (an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-ecmascript-language-types">ECMAScript language value</a></emu-xref>), and <var>leftFirst</var> (a Boolean) and returns either a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">normal completion containing</a></emu-xref> either a Boolean or <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>, or a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#sec-completion-record-specification-type">throw completion</a></emu-xref>. It provides the semantics for the comparison <var>x</var> &lt; <var>y</var>, returning <emu-val>true</emu-val>, <emu-val>false</emu-val>, or <emu-val>undefined</emu-val> (which indicates that the operands could not be coerced to comparable values of the same numeric type). The <var>leftFirst</var> flag is used to control the order in which operations with potentially visible side-effects are performed upon <var>x</var> and <var>y</var>. It is necessary because ECMAScript specifies left to right evaluation of expressions. If <var>leftFirst</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, the <var>x</var> parameter corresponds to an expression that occurs to the left of the <var>y</var> parameter's corresponding expression. If <var>leftFirst</var> is <emu-val>false</emu-val>, the reverse is the case and operations must be performed upon <var>y</var> before <var>x</var>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
      <emu-alg><ol><li>If <var>leftFirst</var> is <emu-val>true</emu-val>, then<ol><li>Let <var>px</var> be ? <emu-xref aoid="ToPrimitive"><a <var>px</var>.</li><li>Let <var>ly</var> be the length of <var>py</var>.</li><li>For each <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#integer"><a <var>ny</var> is <emu-val>NaN</emu-val>, return <emu-val>undefined</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>nx</var> is <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <var>ny</var> is <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <var>nx</var> is <emu-val>+∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub> or <var>ny</var> is <emu-val>-∞</emu-val><sub>𝔽</sub>, return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">If <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>nx</var>) &lt; <emu-xref aoid="ℝ"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#ℝ">ℝ</a></emu-xref>(<var>ny</var>), return <emu-val>true</emu-val>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-val>false</emu-val>.</li></ol></emu-alg>
      <emu-note data-num="1"><span class="note">Note 1</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>Step <emu-xref href="#step-arc-string-check"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#step-arc-string-check">3</a></emu-xref> differs from step <emu-xref href="#step-binary-op-string-check"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#step-binary-op-string-check">1.c</a></emu-xref> in the algorithm that handles the addition operator <code>+</code> (<emu-xref href="#sec-applystringornumericbinaryoperator"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-expressions#sec-applystringornumericbinaryoperator">13.15.3</a></emu-xref>) by using the logical-and operation instead of the logical-or operation.</p>
      </div></emu-note>
      <emu-note data-num="2"><span class="note">Note 2</span><div class="note-contents">
        <p>The comparison of Strings uses a simple lexicographic ordering on sequences of UTF-16 code unit values. There is no attempt to use the more complex, semantically oriented definitions of character or string equality and collating order defined in the Unicode specification. Therefore String values that are canonically equal according to the Unicode Standard but not in the same normalization form could test as unequal. Also note that lexicographic ordering by <em>code unit</em> differs from ordering by <em>code point</em> for Strings containing <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#surrogate-pair"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-data-types-and-values#surrogate-pair">surrogate pairs</a></emu-xref>.</p>
      </div></emu-note>

          <emu-alg><ol><li>Let <var>a</var> be the MV of the first <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a></emu-nt>.</li><li>If the second <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a></emu-nt> is present, then<ol><li>Let <var>b</var> be the MV of the second <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a></emu-nt>.</li><li>Let <var>n</var> be the number of code points in the second <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-DecimalDigits">DecimalDigits</a></emu-nt>.</li></ol></li><li>Else,<ol><li>Let <var>b</var> be 0.</li><li>Let <var>n</var> be 0.</li></ol></li><li>If <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-ExponentPart">ExponentPart</a></emu-nt> is present, let <var>e</var> be the MV of <emu-nt><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/ecmascript-language-lexical-grammar#prod-ExponentPart">ExponentPart</a></emu-nt>; else let <var>e</var> be 0.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="RoundMVResult"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/abstract-operations#sec-roundmvresult">RoundMVResult</a></emu-xref>((<var>a</var> + (<var>b</var> × 10<sup>-<var>n</var></sup>)) × 10<sup><var>e</var></sup>).</li></ol></emu-alg>

120:Tb41,<p>The abstract operation RoundMVResult takes argument <var>n</var> (a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref>) and returns a Number. It converts <var>n</var> to a Number in an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> manner. For the purposes of this abstract operation, a digit is significant if it is not zero or there is a non-zero digit to its left and there is a non-zero digit to its right. For the purposes of this abstract operation, “the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref> denoted by” a representation of a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref> is the inverse of “the decimal representation of” a <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref>. It performs the following steps when called:</p>
          <emu-alg><ol><li>If the decimal representation of <var>n</var> has 20 or fewer significant digits, return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>n</var>).</li><li>Let <var>option1</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref> denoted by the result of replacing each significant digit in the decimal representation of <var>n</var> after the 20th with a 0 digit.</li><li>Let <var>option2</var> be the <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#mathematical-value">mathematical value</a></emu-xref> denoted by the result of replacing each significant digit in the decimal representation of <var>n</var> after the 20th with a 0 digit and then incrementing it at the 20th position (with carrying as necessary).</li><li>Let <var>chosen</var> be an <emu-xref href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/overview#implementation-defined">implementation-defined</a></emu-xref> choice of either <var>option1</var> or <var>option2</var>.</li><li class="exit">Return <emu-xref aoid="𝔽"><a href="/ecma262/nextra-poc/notational-conventions#𝔽">𝔽</a></emu-xref>(<var>chosen</var>).</li></ol></emu-alg>
